# readers — build plan (2026-09-06)

Intent: Build `readers`, one component skill that owns the shared "cold outside read" the loop skills perform today, and convert every skill that brings in a reader to summon it. A reader is a model call whose whole job is to read supplied material and report on it: a fresh Claude subagent, GPT, Gemini, DeepSeek, or Qwen, run read-only against a caller-supplied mandate plus the input documents, with the output captured verbatim. Today that machinery is copied into five skills (inspect, vertical, precon, architect, jpb) with drift between the copies, and three more (signoff, wargame, recheck) spawn fresh Claude reviewers with their own text. For Tony, so that a new model is one roster row in one place, so his last pick per lane is remembered and suggested next time, and so the portable half runs from any harness with a shell (Codex today). Upstream record: `docs/scope/2026-09-05-readers.md` (32 decided lines, settled ground; this plan does not re-decide them) and the four cold reads in `docs/reviews/2026-09-05-precon-cold-read-readers.md`. Tony's words: "strip it out of all five and make one skill that has all of this ability in it, and then those five skills would just summon that skill inside of it. At any time when a new model comes out, we would just update that one component skill that all five are pointing at." Revised 2026-09-06 twice, after two /inspect runs each time (four stamps under Out of scope; the four punch-list blocks under `## Punch list` are the record of what changed and why). Tony's word on the second revision, 2026-09-06: amend in place for every confirmed finding, skip a third inspection, and build.

Constraints:
- Blueprint interview, 2026-09-06, five questions put to Tony with a recommendation each, his answers verbatim: Q1 runner runtime, recommended Python 3 standard library with a bash entry: "agree". Q2 roster format, recommended one JSON file: "agree". Q3 default GPT row, recommended `gpt-5.6-sol` at medium: "astra on low is my default for codex now" (the question was the default GPT row, so `gpt-6-astra` at `low` is the default GPT row for every caller that passes no effort, and `gpt-5.6-sol` stays as a selectable row). Q4 prompting guides as documentation, not runtime text, GPT guide adapted from the Codex plugin's recipe: "agree". Q5 fleets allowed (one summon may carry several calls): "agree". Two later rulings the same day: precon's exit test may run several readers on Tony's word ("open 1: yes"); reasoning is on by default for the DeepSeek and Qwen rows ("on by default"). Five rulings at the second revision, 2026-09-06: amend and build without a third inspection ("yes"); a GPT reader that can write a file under the read-only sandbox stops the lane rather than shipping with a label ("go with your rec", Slice A R9); a typed model id inherits the named row's envelope (assumption 11, "yes"); the memory-note update in Slice I stays ("yes"); architect and recheck get live installed proofs, wargame and vertical stay deferred ("yes").
- Stack: Markdown skills under `plugins/`, plus one new runner: Python 3 standard library only, compatible with Python 3.9 (`/usr/bin/python3` on this Mac is 3.9.6 and is what a bare PATH resolves), no third-party packages, no npm step, with a bash entry point that execs it. The roster is one JSON file read by both the runner and the skill body. No package.json, no test runner at the repo root; verification is shell checks, the runner's own pre-send `validate` step, fresh-session reads, and live runs recorded under `docs/evidence/readers/`.
- Source of truth (repo `AGENTS.md`): the copy a session runs is the installed plugin under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/tony-skills/<plugin>/<version>/` (the version directory is the first twelve hex characters of the commit it was installed from, e.g. precon at `2baaecba12a1` is commit 2baaecb), not this checkout. A change reaches a running machine only after it lands on `main` AND that machine runs `/plugin install readers@tony-skills` (first time, both Macs) or `/plugin update <name>@tony-skills`. Consequence for this plan: every proof that needs an installed caller (a live /precon, /inspect, /jpb, /signoff, /architect, or /recheck run through readers) lives in Slice I, the carrier for post-install evidence, never in the caller slice's own PR. That deferral is Tony's ruling at the first revision (the alternative, proving each conversion live inside its own PR, is impossible before install). Caller slices D–H prove their conversion pre-merge by greps, a fresh-session read, and the request-validate check (each slice's ACs), and Slice C proves its host lanes pre-merge from the checkout with `claude --plugin-dir plugins/readers` (legal here because no installed copy of readers exists yet to collide with; the rule against `--plugin-dir` for fresh reads of edited skills, `docs/plans/2026-09-05-sun-live-run-fixes-2.md:7`, stands for every other plugin).
- Paths: readers' assets live at `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/` and a SKILL.md reaches them as `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/readers/assets/...` because in this repo `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is the plugin root (jpb's SKILL.md:31 uses `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/jpb/assets/`; sun keeps its assets at the plugin root and uses `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/assets/`; `AGENTS.md:41` names only the second form and is widened in Slice I to name both). A shell-only harness (Codex) runs the checkout's copy by absolute path, `~/Developer/tony-skills/plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers`, because Codex has no plugin cache. Never an absolute `~/.claude/...` path inside a SKILL.md (repo invariant).
- Access profiles are the caller's parameter (scope doc): `starved` (a fresh empty directory, nothing to read), `packet-only` (a fresh directory the runner creates holding copies of the request's documents and nothing else; on rows whose model has no filesystem, the OpenRouter rows and the Claude rows under the Workflow route, the documents travel only in the prompt and the sidecar records the profile as `packet-only (no workspace)`), `repo` (a caller-supplied workspace to read: vertical's export, inspect's repo-reality walk), `repo-with-tools` (a workspace the reviewer may also run tests in, writes confined to scratch and ignored caches, never a tracked file; execution blocked by the sandbox is reported as "verification blocked", never marked checked — scope doc Round 4 Q4; a lens whose method must mutate the checkout asks for `isolation: worktree`, below). Each roster row lists the profiles it supports; a request naming an unsupported profile is refused before any send with status `profile-unsupported`. The scope doc's packet-only inversion for inspect binds its outside lanes; inspect's repo-reality lens already walks the repo as local Claude today (inspect SKILL.md:48) and keeps doing so under the `repo` profile.
- Outside means any lane that leaves the Anthropic account (GPT, Gemini, DeepSeek, Qwen). An outside lane runs only with the caller's `authorized` flag, which is the caller's evidence of Tony's word in that run; Claude rows never need it. One authorization covers one named logical reader invocation; a fleet authorizes exactly the calls it enumerates (jpb's approval therefore enumerates its six boxes and its Judge G call); a retry or replacement needs the word again; internal tool turns inside a tool-using review never count (scope doc Round 3 Q2, Round 4 Q5). `readers` never adds a lane question when summoned by a caller; each caller's existing ask policy governs. The window between the ask and the send is closed by the freeze: the `suggest` step that feeds a caller's ask also writes the run's snapshot, and every later call with that run id resolves from it (Slice B).
- Secrets: `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is read from the environment into the request's `Authorization` header and nowhere else: never persisted, never logged, never printed, never in a sidecar, diagnostics file, or evidence file (the adapter redacts the header from anything it writes). Existence is checked with `[ -n "$OPENROUTER_API_KEY" ] && echo set` and nothing else. The never-read-`~/.zshrc` rule (the key lives there beside an alias; a read lands it in a transcript, and one did on 2026-08-30 after the 2026-08-11 leak forced a rotation, per inspect SKILL.md:58 and jpb's box-runners.md:24) moves into readers' contract as the one place it is stated. The repo is PUBLIC: no key, no sample credential, nothing Tony-specific added without a deliberate call.
- Model floors: signoff, wargame, and recheck run reviewers at Opus-class or better; vertical's local review runs under signoff's full reviewer mechanics (vertical SKILL.md:53) and so inherits the same floor. `readers` enforces a floor the caller passes: a row or pick below it, or an id it cannot classify, is refused with a status and the caller decides; `readers` never silently upgrades. Eligibility is a roster field Tony changes by PR (scope doc Round 4 Q3); Fable and Opus are at or above the floor per wargame SKILL.md:12 ("Opus, Fable/Mythos, or any successor tier above Opus"). The session-inheriting Claude row is judged by the session's own model id, which only the skill body knows; it passes that id in the request (Slice A R2 `session_model`), and a floor-bound call whose request carries none is refused.
- Git gate: feature branch per slice, local commits fine, stop before `git push` and `gh pr create`; Tony says "PR" then "merge" per slice. One PR per slice. Never push to `main`. Slice A's PR also carries the upstream files this plan rests on, added as they stand: the four untracked docs (`docs/scope/2026-09-05-readers.md`, `docs/reviews/2026-09-05-precon-cold-read-readers.md`, this plan, `docs/reviews/2026-09-06-inspect-readers-gpt.md`), the second astra raw file `docs/reviews/2026-09-06-inspect-readers-gpt-2.md`, and the modified `docs/feedback.md` (its uncommitted Inbox tail holds the three `readers` notes Slice D dispositions), so every later branch has the record.
- The `AGENTS.md` edit in Slice I follows `~/ObsidianVault/01-domain/agents-md-best-practices.md` (Tony's global rule for any AGENTS.md edit); read it before editing.
- Live-run spend, stated as counts of model calls that actually send: Slice A ten GPT calls (AC2 one, AC6 one authorized nested run plus the escalation case when a prompt appears, AC7 two, R9 four isolation runs, AC12 none: it refuses before sending); Slice B seven OpenRouter calls (AC1 two, AC5 one, AC7 two, AC10 two; AC2–AC4, AC6, AC8 send nothing); Slice C one Gemini call and two GPT calls (AC4 Gemini, AC8 one GPT, AC6 rides AC4's Gemini run; AC10 sends nothing); Slice I re-proves the installed host lanes (one Claude, one Gemini), runs one live /inspect on this plan in the Claude lane, one live /precon exit test and one live /architect review and one live /recheck on the Claude lane, and jpb's six-box smoke on Tony's word only. Every live send in this plan reads the fixture `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/fixtures/smoke-doc.md` (under 2 KB) except Slice I's /inspect (this plan), its /precon, /architect, and /recheck runs (throwaway docs in a scratch repo), and the jpb smoke (jpb's own `assets/fixtures/smoke-brief.md`); the oversize checks (A AC3, B AC4) generate their documents and never send. Truncation, finish-reason, and guard-order checks run on canned transport responses for both the OpenRouter and the GPT adapter (Slice B R8), not live calls.
- Fresh-session reads are plain `claude -p` from the repo root told to read the file at its checkout path (the convention this repo's build docs use, e.g. `docs/plans/2026-09-05-sun-live-run-fixes-2.md:7`), never `--plugin-dir` for an already-installed plugin.
- Blueprint assumptions (small reversible calls made here, visible to builder and inspector): (1) `$READERS_CHECKOUT` names the tony-skills checkout root, default `~/Developer/tony-skills`; the last-pick memory file is `$READERS_CHECKOUT/plugins/readers/last-picks.json`; the roster is always read from the directory the running `readers.py` sits in, never from `$READERS_CHECKOUT`; the installed cache copy of the memory file is never read or written; acceptance runs that exercise memory point `$READERS_CHECKOUT` at a scratch clone of the checkout so the tracked file never carries test residue. (2) Claude pinned rows carry the harness model names `fable` and `opus` (what the Agent and Workflow tools accept, as jpb's `claude-boxes.workflow.js:21–22` does), not API ids. (3) Every call has an output directory `<run dir>/<call id>/` holding `sidecar.json`, `raw.md`, and its diagnostics; the run directory is resolved the same way by every step (`suggest`, `validate`, dispatch): the request's `run_dir` or `suggest`'s `--run-dir`, else `$READERS_RUN_ROOT/<run id>`, else `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/readers/<run id>`; a caller-named raw path receives a copy of `raw.md`, so the sidecar and the immutable capture never depend on the caller's directory. The run-dir `raw.md` is evidence, distinct from the filed copy the scope doc leaves to the caller's filing policy (scope doc Round 4 Q1): a fileless caller such as inspect's Claude lane files nothing under `docs/` and reads `raw_text`. (4) Field and status names are fixed once by Slice A's `contract.md`; later slices cite that file rather than restating names. (5) jpb's boxes and Judge G use the roster's default GPT row (`gpt-astra`) unless Tony picks otherwise at jpb's approval ask, and jpb passes effort `high` to every row that accepts an effort (Claude and GPT), as its boxes are pinned high today; the astra-low default is for callers that pass no effort. (6) `box-runners.md` becomes a one-paragraph pointer to readers rather than being deleted, so links from `docs/` keep resolving; jpb's `openrouter-box.sh` and `claude-boxes.workflow.js` are removed once readers carries their behaviour. (7) inspect's repo-reality lens uses the `repo` profile; signoff, recheck, and wargame's adversary agents use `repo-with-tools`. (8) The `codex exec` read-only sandbox's isolation strength is whatever Slice A's sentinel test measures, never an assumed packet-only guarantee; a sandbox that lets the reader write stops the lane (Tony, second revision). (9) Claude calls under `starved` and `packet-only`, and any Claude call with a pinned effort, run through the Workflow route (jpb's committed `claude-boxes.workflow.js` pattern, which records a per-agent tool-call count the parity line needs); `repo` and `repo-with-tools` calls with no effort run as plain `general-purpose` subagents that inherit the session model, or pin `fable`/`opus` when the row does; when the Workflow tool is absent from the session, a call that needs it is `lane-unavailable` with reason `Workflow tool absent`. (10) `gpt-sol`'s default effort is `medium`, a roster default chosen here (no caller passes an effort today); `~/.codex/models_cache.json` lists the reverse defaults (`default_reasoning_level` medium for gpt-6-astra, low for gpt-5.6-sol), so both rows' `quirks` say the roster overrides the cache's default on purpose. (11) A typed model id inherits the envelope of the row it was named against (context window, max output, effort encoding, supported profiles), recorded in the sidecar as `envelope: inherited from <row id>`; eligibility stays `not classified` (Tony, second revision: "yes"). (12) Judge K runs as one `claude-session` call at effort `high`, profile `starved`, mirroring its current fresh-subagent-at-high shape (jpb SKILL.md:185–194); the scope doc says only that judges route through readers. (13) Order inside the C-dependent slices: E lands before G, because G deletes `openrouter-box.sh` and E removes inspect's reference to it; D and F have no ordering constraint.

Out of scope:
- Grok, on any transport — abandoned by Tony (scope doc). Slice G removes it from jpb's instruction text and code (SKILL.md, plugin.json, the marketplace description, `judge-mandate.md`'s Grok down-weight rule, `render-page.py`, `jpb-vendor-styles.json`; the recommendation to put all of them in G and retire the down-weight rule with Grok was adopted by Tony 2026-09-06: "fix all five as recommended"). Records keep their history: `docs/`, and jpb's `assets/fixtures/` (the 2026-08-09 smoke run), are never edited for this and every sweep exempts them.
- Local Qwen on Ollama — dropped by Tony (scope doc); Slice E removes inspect's Ollama recipe.
- A Gemini CLI or API transport for shell-only harnesses — Gemini is a host lane through the antigravity MCP; a Codex-hosted caller gets `lane-unavailable` for it (scope doc, Round 2 close-out line on native transports; Slice A R2 and AC12 make the shell entry say so).
- jpb's scrubber, box validator, three-valid-boxes rule, and verdict — caller post-processing that stays in jpb (scope doc Round 2 C); its cost fetch and page render also stay, as jpb-only post-processing this plan touches only where Qwen or Grok force it (Slice G).
- Run-time "latest model" resolution — a roster row moves only by PR on Tony's word (scope doc Round 2 A).
- A generalized plugin dependency manager, a comprehensive model-ranking system, telemetry beyond the evidence sidecar — the Codex review's "Can wait" list at `docs/reviews/2026-09-05-precon-cold-read-readers.md:658`; an explicit protocol-version check and an eligibility field suffice.
- Removing the `codex mcp-server` entry from `~/.claude.json` inside any PR — it is per-machine config, done on Tony's word as a manual step after every caller is migrated (scope doc constraint f); Slice I lists it.
- Bumping the Claude pinned rows or any other roster id beyond the values this plan fixes — a roster change is its own PR on Tony's word (scope doc Round 1 Q12), never a side effect of a build slice.
- Any change to build, blueprint, ship, handoff, huh, fb, digest, sun, forge, arcade, print-tune, or shutdown — they bring in no reader (scope doc caller-set line; grep of all 21 SKILL.md files, 2026-09-06).
- The OpenAI Codex plugin (`codex@openai-codex`, installed at `~/.claude/plugins/cache/openai-codex/codex/1.0.6/`) and its skills — untouched, because its prompting skill is written for coding tasks and changes with that plugin's updates; readers' GPT guide is its own stable file adapted from it.
- Behavioural proof of caller conversions inside their own PRs — impossible before install (Source of truth above); it lives in Slice I by design, with the pre-merge request-validate check as each caller slice's own proof.
- Live installed proofs for wargame and vertical — wargame is a heavy user-invoked run with no throwaway target, and vertical needs a fully signed-off build; both are deferred to Tony's call after this plan's own slices are signed off (Tony, second revision). Architect and recheck get live proofs in Slice I.
- Re-slicing — nine slices stand; a two-inspection MINOR that D and F could share a PR is declined so each caller conversion stays one reviewable PR.

## Slice A — the contract, the roster, and the GPT lane
Goal: A runner at a real path executes one cold read on GPT through `codex exec` under a written request/result contract whose pre-send checks refuse bad requests without spend, with the roster file complete, the isolation of that lane measured rather than assumed, and the nested-run and denied-run cases proven from Tony's Codex window.
Requirements:
- R1: Plugin scaffold: `plugins/readers/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` with the same keys as `plugins/precon/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (name `readers`, Tony as author, description) and both `homepage` and `repository` set to `https://github.com/line7works/tony-skills`, the org of `git remote -v` (precon's file and 18 others still say `tiny-tunnel-dot`; not copied), plus the asset folder `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/`. No SKILL.md and no marketplace entry yet (Slice C); the plugin is not installable until then.
- R2: The contract, `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/contract.md`, fixes once the names later slices cite. A request carries: `protocol_version` (the version the caller was written against); `run_id` (the caller's run); `call_id` (caller-supplied, or minted by the runner when absent and returned in the result); `run_dir` (optional; assumption 3 gives the default); the roster `row`; the `mandate` (text or a file path); `documents` (file paths) and/or `workspace` (a directory) with the access `profile`; optional `effort`; optional `model` override (a typed id); optional `output_budget` (an integer never above the row's max output); optional `raw_path` the caller owns; the `authorized` flag; optional `floor`; optional `session_model` (the harness model id of the session that summoned a `claude-session` call, filled by the skill body, empty from a shell); optional `isolation` (`worktree`, for a `repo-with-tools` Claude call whose method mutates the checkout; the Claude lane then launches the reviewer with the Agent tool's worktree isolation and the sidecar records it). A result carries every field below in every sidecar, null when not applicable, so the every-field checks (AC2, C AC9) have one shape: `status`; `row`, `override_source` (roster default, remembered pick, explicit pick), `requested_model`, `effective_model`, `requested_effort`, `effective_effort`, `envelope` (the row whose limits applied), `budget_method` (the estimate used, or `window-only` when the row's max output is unknown, or `skipped (window unknown)`); `protocol_version` and `adapter_version`; `mandate_hash` and `packet_hash`; `profile`, `workdir`, `isolation` (the row's label for the profile used); `parity`; `raw_text` (the verbatim capture on `ok`, the empty string on any other status), `raw_file` (`<run dir>/<call id>/raw.md`, null unless `ok`), `raw_hash` (null unless `ok`), `raw_path` when the caller named one (null otherwise); `diagnostics` (the directory; an `incomplete` call's partial text is at `<diagnostics>/partial.md` and nowhere else); `generation_id` and `response_raw` (null unless the transport provides them, Slice B); `started_at`, `ended_at`, `duration_s`. Statuses, each a backticked term on its own bulleted line in the contract: `ok` · `empty` (no content or whitespace only) · `incomplete` (truncated: finish reason, output cap, or interrupted stream) · `oversize` (rejected before any send, with the measurements) · `invalid-request` (malformed JSON, a missing required field, an unknown row id, an effort the row does not list, an output budget above the row's max or not an integer) · `lane-unavailable` (transport or credential absent, the Workflow tool absent for a call that needs it, or a `host` row requested through the shell entry, which cannot run one; reason text names which) · `unauthorized` (outside row without the flag) · `floor-refused` (a row or pick whose eligibility is `not eligible`, or a `claude-session` call whose `session_model` is absent or not on the roster's `eligible_session_models` list, when a floor is passed) · `unknown-model` (floor-bound call, id `not classified`) · `profile-unsupported` (the row does not support the requested profile) · `version-mismatch` (request `protocol_version` differs from the runner's) · `transport-failed` (with the transport's own error) · `capture-failed` (the model answered but `raw.md` could not be written) · `cancelled` (the caller interrupted the runner; the child is killed) · `timed-out` (the row's `timeout_s` elapsed; the child is killed; beats `incomplete`). Pre-send checks run in this order on every call, host or portable, and any failure returns before a child is launched or a request sent: request validity, version, authorization, profile support, floor and classification, lane availability, budget. The shell entry exits 0 only on `ok` and prints the result as JSON on stdout in every case, including `invalid-request`. `readers --version` prints the protocol version, which starts at `1`; `readers validate <request>` (a file path, or `-` for stdin) runs only the pre-send checks and prints `valid` or the refusing status, never dispatching; validate reads the named documents to size them, so a request with a missing document is `invalid-request`.
- R3: The roster, `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/roster.json`: `protocol_version` at the top, `eligible_session_models` (id patterns that satisfy the Opus floor for `claude-session`: `claude-opus-*`, `claude-fable-*`, `claude-mythos-*`, per wargame SKILL.md:12), and one row per lane with these fields, all present on every row: `id`; `provider`; `transport` (`codex-exec`, `openrouter`, `antigravity-mcp`, `claude-subagent`); `kind` (`portable` or `host`); `model` (the default id); `effort_encoding` (`flag`, `suffix`, `field`, or `unsupported`), `effort_default`, `efforts` (the supported values); `context_window` and `max_output` (integers, or the literal `unknown` with a `limits_source` naming where the builder looked); `timeout_s`; `credential_env` (or empty); `web_off` (the mechanism) and `parity` (an object keyed by profile, each value the exact line to record for that profile, because today's Gemini lines differ per caller: jpb's neutral cwd, inspect's packet dir, vertical's export dir); `supported_profiles`; `eligibility` (`eligible`, `not eligible`, `session-dependent`, or `not classified`); `isolation` (an object keyed by profile; every value `unmeasured` until a slice measures it: Slice A R9 for the GPT rows, Slice C AC5/AC6 for the Claude and Gemini rows; the OpenRouter rows are `no-workspace` under both supported profiles because the model has no filesystem); `available` (true, or false with a reason; R9 can set it false); `reasoning` (on/off, OpenRouter rows); `quirks` (text); `guide` (empty until Slice I); `verified_at`. Initial rows: `gpt-astra` = `gpt-6-astra`, effort `low`, the default GPT row (blueprint Q3); `gpt-sol` = `gpt-5.6-sol`, effort `medium` (assumption 10); `gemini` = `gemini-3.1-pro-high`, effort in the model-name suffix, never also passed as a parameter; `deepseek` = `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro-0813` (1,048,576 context, 384,000 max output, reasoning on); `qwen` = `qwen/qwen3.8-max-0902` (1,000,000 context, 131,072 max output, reasoning on) — both ids and limits from the scope doc's 2026-09-06 OpenRouter lookup, one release ahead of the `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro` the callers pin today; `claude-session` (inherits the session model; eligibility `session-dependent`); `claude-fable` = `fable`; `claude-opus` = `opus`. GPT rows take `context_window` from `~/.codex/models_cache.json` (`context_window`, 272000 for both on 2026-09-06; the source jpb's preflight reads, SKILL.md:56) and record `max_output` as `unknown` with `limits_source` "models_cache.json carries no output-token field (checked 2026-09-06)"; Gemini's are read from antigravity `list_models` in Slice C, when the host lane is built (Tony, 2026-09-06 signoff ruling: "defer to slice c"); until then the row carries `unknown` for both with a `limits_source` naming that deferral; Claude rows record both as `unknown` with source `harness`. Supported profiles: portable rows `starved`, `packet-only`, and (GPT only) `repo`; Claude rows all four; Gemini `starved`, `packet-only`, `repo`. Eligibility: `claude-fable` and `claude-opus` `eligible` (wargame SKILL.md:12); `claude-session` `session-dependent`; outside rows `not classified`.
- R4: The runner, `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers.py`, with the bash entry `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers` (executable, execs `python3` on the sibling script, resolves its own location, depends on no alias, startup file, or Claude-only environment variable). It reads a request from a JSON file or stdin, runs the R2 pre-send checks, resolves the row and any explicit override (remembered picks arrive in Slice B), measures the composed prompt (mandate, delimiters, and documents together; the estimate is bytes divided by 3.5, the method inspect uses today at SKILL.md:52, plus ten percent headroom, recorded as `budget_method`) against the row's context window minus the output budget (the request's `output_budget`, else the row's `max_output`, else nothing when that is `unknown`, recorded as `window-only`; a row whose window is `unknown` skips the check, recorded as `skipped (window unknown)`) and rejects oversize before any send, composes the prompt as the mandate at the top followed by each document delimited as evidence (a semantic migration from the MCP's `base-instructions`; nothing is placed in Codex's instructions-file setting), builds the packet directory for `packet-only` by copying the documents into a fresh directory, and dispatches the GPT adapter. Document bytes reach the child through stdin or files, never through generated shell source, argv, or `eval`. Before any child launch or request, the runner appends one line to `<run dir>/<call id>/dispatch.log`; a refused call never creates that file. A `host` row reaching dispatch through the shell entry is refused as `lane-unavailable` (validate still validates it, so a caller can check a host request from a shell).
- R5: The GPT adapter runs `codex exec` as a fresh child: the row's model and effort passed explicitly; sandbox read-only; `--skip-git-repo-check` (codex-cli 0.153.4 refuses to run outside a git repository without it, and `starved` and `packet-only` directories are not repositories); web search off by an explicit override, `-c web_search=disabled` (the config key the MCP route accepts as `{"web_search": "disabled"}`; the top-level `--search` flag is never passed and there is no exec-level switch), recorded as the parity line `web_search: disabled (-c web_search=disabled)`; the working directory per profile (a fresh empty directory for `starved`, the packet directory for `packet-only`, the caller's workspace for `repo`; `repo-with-tools` is `profile-unsupported` on this row); the prompt on stdin; the final response captured through the CLI's output-file option, the event stream and stderr to separate diagnostics files; never `resume`, `--last`, or `fork`; a fresh output directory per call so a stale file from an earlier attempt is never read; the child killed at the row's `timeout_s`. The evidence records whether the child's working directory carried an instruction file (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`) the child could read.
- R6: Guards, applied in this order and each mapped to a contract status: the child's exit code and startup failure (`transport-failed`, with the CLI's message such as `failed to initialize in-process app-server client: Operation not permitted` preserved verbatim); a truncation signal from the event stream or output cap (`incomplete`, with any partial text kept at `<diagnostics>/partial.md` only, never as `raw.md` or `raw_text`); empty content (`empty`); a response that cannot be written to `raw.md` (`capture-failed`). Transport success and content status are separate fields.
- R7: Evidence: `sidecar.json` per call in `<run dir>/<call id>/`, holding every result field from R2, written before the result is returned and never rewritten; `raw.md` beside it is the exact final response text and `raw_hash` covers it; a caller-named `raw_path` receives a copy, and when that path already exists the runner appends `-2`, `-3` to the filename rather than overwriting (inspect SKILL.md:60 and architect SKILL.md:114 use the same rule). What a caller does to its copy (inspect's banner, precon's disposition) never touches `raw.md` or the hash.
- R8: Concurrency: two calls running at once never share an output directory, packet directory, or diagnostics file; a caller-supplied `workspace` is shared read-only by design and is not a violation. Run-scoped names come from `run_id` plus `call_id`. jpb's parallel fleet depends on this from Slice G.
- R9: Isolation measured, not claimed, for the GPT lane under `starved` and `packet-only`: a sentinel file with a known string is placed outside the packet directory and the reader is asked to report its contents; a second run asks the reader to create a file in its working directory. Four outcomes are recorded in `docs/evidence/readers/slice-a-isolation.md`. Per profile, the row's `isolation` label is `sandbox-enforced` when the sentinel read failed and the write was denied, `instruction-only (read)` when the sentinel was read but the write was denied. A write that succeeds under either profile stops the lane (Tony, second revision): both GPT rows get `available: false` with reason `sandbox let the reader write (<date>)`, every request on them returns `lane-unavailable` until a later roster PR on Tony's word records a denial, and AC2, AC6, AC7, and AC12 are recorded as blocked in the evidence rather than passed. `gpt-sol` shares the transport and sandbox and takes the same labels, recorded as `inherited from gpt-astra`. Every result carries the label for the profile used so a caller's stamp can name it (inspect and vertical do from Slices E and F).
- R10: The nested case from Tony's Codex window, with the real runner from the checkout: an authorized read of the fixture completes (status `ok`) while the parent Codex thread is live under Tony's current auto-review setting; a refused case terminates cleanly with a structured status and no `dispatch.log` (the runner invoked without `authorized`); the scope doc's denied case (Round 3 Q4: the parent denies the child's escalation and no fallback send happens) is run whenever Tony's window offers an escalation prompt, with him declining it; when no prompt appears under auto-review the evidence says "no escalation prompt appeared; the denied case is not producible under auto-review" and names that as a standing limit. Results, the parent's approval behaviour observed, and the auto-review setting in force (`grep -E 'approval_policy|approvals_reviewer' ~/.codex/config.toml`, or the words "neither key set") go to `docs/evidence/readers/slice-a-nested.md`. This supersedes the one-word test recorded in the scope doc, whose limits that doc states.
- R11: The fixture `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/fixtures/smoke-doc.md`: a self-contained document under 2 KB with at least one deliberate ambiguity a cold reader can name, used by every live send in this plan except those named in Constraints.
- R12: The runner never persists, logs, or prints a credential value: a credential is read from the environment into a request header and nowhere else, headers are redacted from diagnostics, and the runner never reads `~/.zshrc` (the contract states this rule in its own words).
Acceptance criteria:
- AC1: The entry point runs from a plain shell on the system Python — verify: `env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=$HOME plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers --version` prints `1` (this resolves `/usr/bin/python3`, 3.9.6, so it doubles as the version-floor check).
- AC2: A GPT read of the fixture returns ok with capture and evidence — verify: manual: run the entry with a request naming row `gpt-astra`, the fixture as the document, profile `starved`, `authorized` set, a `raw_path` under the scratchpad; the JSON result shows status `ok`, `raw_text` is non-empty, `<run dir>/<call id>/raw.md` and the `raw_path` copy are byte-identical to the CLI's own output file (`cmp` prints nothing for both pairs), and `sidecar.json` holds every R2 result field: `python3 -c "import json,sys;s=json.load(open(sys.argv[1]));print([k for k in sys.argv[2].split(',') if k not in s])" <sidecar> status,row,override_source,requested_model,effective_model,requested_effort,effective_effort,envelope,budget_method,protocol_version,adapter_version,mandate_hash,packet_hash,profile,workdir,isolation,parity,raw_text,raw_file,raw_hash,raw_path,diagnostics,generation_id,response_raw,started_at,ended_at,duration_s` prints `[]`; the command, the result JSON, and the sidecar are recorded in `docs/evidence/readers/slice-a-gpt-run.md`.
- AC3: Pre-send refusals never dispatch — verify: seven requests on the fixture, one per refusal, every one carrying `authorized` except the first so that only the check under test fails: (1) no `authorized` on `gpt-astra` → `unauthorized`; (2) row `claude-session`, `floor: opus`, `session_model: claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` → `floor-refused`; (3) `gpt-astra`, `floor: opus`, `model: some-new-id` → `unknown-model`; (4) `gpt-astra`, profile `repo-with-tools` → `profile-unsupported`; (5) `protocol_version: 0` → `version-mismatch`; (6) row `no-such-row` → `invalid-request`; (7) `gpt-astra` with a document larger than the row's window, generated with `python3 -c "print('x'*N)"` where N is the window times 4 → `oversize`, with the measured size and the limit in the result. Each exits non-zero with that status, and for each `ls <run dir>/<call id>` prints `sidecar.json` alone (no `dispatch.log`, no diagnostics).
- AC4: The validate step matches the dispatch path — verify: `readers validate` on AC2's request prints `valid`; on each AC3 request it prints the same status AC3 produced.
- AC5: Isolation is measured and recorded — verify: `docs/evidence/readers/slice-a-isolation.md` names all four outcomes (sentinel read and write attempt, under `starved` and `packet-only`) and the resulting per-profile labels; `python3 -c "import json;r=json.load(open('plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/roster.json'));x=[x for x in r['rows'] if x['id']=='gpt-astra'][0];print(x['isolation'],x['available'])"` prints those labels and `True` (a `False` here means the lane was stopped per R9 and the slice records the blocked ACs); AC2's sidecar carries the `starved` label.
- AC6: The nested and denied cases are proven from Tony's Codex window — verify: manual: Tony pastes the two commands the builder prepares (the checkout's entry point with a request file) into his Codex window; `docs/evidence/readers/slice-a-nested.md` records the authorized and refused results verbatim, the denied case or the words "no escalation prompt appeared; the denied case is not producible under auto-review", and the config grep output or "neither key set".
- AC7: Concurrent calls do not collide — verify: launch two authorized fixture reads in the background with the same `run_id` and different `call_id`s, `wait`, then `find <run dir> -name sidecar.json | wc -l` prints `2` and the two `raw.md` files sit in different directories.
- AC8: No credential leaks — verify: `grep -rin 'zshrc' plugins/readers/ | grep -viEc 'never|not '` prints `0` (every mention is the prohibition); `grep -rEn 'sk-or-[A-Za-z0-9]' plugins/readers/ docs/evidence/readers/` prints nothing; `grep -rc 'Authorization' <AC2 diagnostics dir>` prints `0` for every file.
- AC9: The roster is complete — verify: `python3 -c "import json;r=json.load(open('plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/roster.json'));print(sorted(x['id'] for x in r['rows']))"` prints exactly `['claude-fable', 'claude-opus', 'claude-session', 'deepseek', 'gemini', 'gpt-astra', 'gpt-sol', 'qwen']`; a second `python3 -c` check that every row has every R3 field (the field list pasted from R3) prints `[]`; `gpt-astra`'s `effort_default` is `low`, its `context_window` is the integer `272000` and its `max_output` is `unknown` with a non-empty `limits_source`; `deepseek`'s model is `deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro-0813`, `qwen`'s is `qwen/qwen3.8-max-0902` with `context_window` `1000000`, both with `reasoning` on; `eligible_session_models` is a non-empty list.
- AC10: The contract defines every status — verify: for each of the sixteen status names in R2, `grep -c '^- \`<status>\`' plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/contract.md` prints `1`.
- AC11: The oversize estimate is stated — verify: `grep -c '3.5' plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/contract.md` prints 1 or more, and AC3's oversize result names `budget_method`.
- AC12: A host row from the shell is honest — verify: a request naming row `gemini` (authorized, fixture, `starved`) through the entry exits non-zero with status `lane-unavailable` and a reason naming the host lane, and `ls <run dir>/<call id>` prints `sidecar.json` alone; `readers validate` on the same request prints `valid`.
Footprint: plugins/readers/.claude-plugin/plugin.json; plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/contract.md; plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/roster.json; plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers; plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers.py; plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/fixtures/smoke-doc.md; docs/evidence/readers/slice-a-gpt-run.md; docs/evidence/readers/slice-a-isolation.md; docs/evidence/readers/slice-a-nested.md; plus the upstream files named in Constraints (Git gate), added as they stand.
Not in this slice: the OpenRouter adapter, the memory file, the run-wide freeze, the SKILL.md, the marketplace entry, the prompting guides, any caller change.
Depends on: nothing
Status: signed off

## Slice B — OpenRouter lanes, budgets, memory, and the run-wide freeze
Goal: DeepSeek and Qwen run through the same runner with the output budget and the whole-prompt input check the old script lacked, Tony's last pick per row is remembered in the checkout and suggested next time, and every call in one caller run sees the same frozen roster and memory from the moment the caller's ask is built.
Requirements:
- R1: The OpenRouter adapter serves the `deepseek` and `qwen` rows under `starved` and `packet-only` (documents travel as text in the prompt; `repo` and `repo-with-tools` are `profile-unsupported` on these rows): the chat-completions endpoint with the row's or override's model id, the credential from `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` in the request header only, an output budget from the request's `output_budget` when given, else the row's `max_output`, reasoning on by default (Tony, 2026-09-06) the way the provider accepts for these ids (verified live once, the exact parameter recorded in the row's quirks), and no `:online` suffix, recorded as the parity line `no :online suffix`. A missing credential is caught by the pre-send lane-availability check and returns `lane-unavailable` with no `dispatch.log`, never a crash. The raw HTTP response body is saved as `<run dir>/<call id>/response.raw` and its path returned as `response_raw`, so jpb's cost script (which reads a raw response's top-level `id`) keeps working unchanged.
- R2: Guards for this transport, each mapped to a contract status: HTTP 200 with no top-level error and finish reason `stop` is `ok`; finish reason `length` is `incomplete` with the partial text at `<diagnostics>/partial.md` only; empty or whitespace content is `empty`; any other finish reason (a tool call, a content filter) is `transport-failed` with the reason preserved; any other HTTP status or a parse failure is `transport-failed` with the provider's message. When two conditions coexist the earlier guard in this order wins (a truncated body with empty content is `incomplete`). The generation id from the response is recorded as `generation_id`.
- R3: Budget accounting for every row, not only DeepSeek, by the rule Slice A R4 fixes (bytes divided by 3.5 plus ten percent headroom; a provider tokenizer may replace it when available and the method is recorded as `budget_method`): the composed prompt (mandate, delimiters, documents) is compared with the row's or envelope's context window minus the output budget; oversize is rejected before any send with the measurement and the limit in the result; a row with an `unknown` max output checks window-only; a row with an `unknown` window skips the check and says so. The packet is never truncated or summarized to fit.
- R4: The last-pick memory, `plugins/readers/last-picks.json` in the checkout (assumption 1), created by this slice as `{"protocol_version": 1, "picks": {}}` and committed so the tracked file the scope doc decided exists on `main`: keyed per roster row id; an entry records the picked model id, the date, and the roster row's default model id at the time of the pick; updates are atomic (read, modify, write to a temporary file, rename; the builder chooses the exclusion mechanism) so concurrent fleet calls with explicit picks never lose each other's entries; written when a call is dispatched with an explicit pick; read by the `suggest` step. When the checkout is absent or unwritable, the result reports `memory: unavailable` and the roster row is used; nothing is written anywhere else.
- R5: The `suggest` step (`readers suggest <row id>[,<row id>...] --run <run id> [--run-dir <dir>] [--floor <floor>]`): for each row, returns the remembered pick when one exists and is still usable, else the roster default, plus whether the row is outside (needs Tony's word) and any drop note, and, on its first call for a run id, writes that run's snapshot of the roster and memory (R6) so the model the caller shows Tony is the model the run sends. A remembered pick is dropped with a note, at suggest time and never after: when the roster row's default has changed since the pick (a roster PR bumped the row); when the id no longer exists where that can be checked (OpenRouter's model list for `deepseek` and `qwen`; `~/.codex/models_cache.json` for GPT rows); or when it sits below the passed floor. A drop is recorded in the memory file so the note appears once.
- R6: Run-wide freeze: the first `suggest` or dispatch carrying a run id snapshots the roster and the memory file into `<run dir>/snapshot/` (the run dir resolved per assumption 3, so a caller that passes `run_dir` to its calls passes the same directory as `--run-dir` to `suggest`), created exclusively so two concurrent first calls produce one snapshot (the loser reads the winner's); every later call with that run id resolves rows and picks from the snapshot, never the live files; the sidecar names the snapshot it used; the snapshot plus each call's sidecar (`override_source`, `effective_model`, `effective_effort`) together are the scope doc's run record (Round 2 Q12, its assumed line on snapshot contents).
- R7: The typed-id rule from the scope doc: on a call with no floor, an explicit id absent from the roster is sent as-is under the named row's envelope (assumption 11) and remembered; on a floor-bound call it returns `unknown-model`.
- R8: Transport test hooks, refused (status `transport-failed`, reason `canned response outside test`) unless `READERS_TEST=1` is also set, with the sidecar recording that a canned response was used: `READERS_CANNED_RESPONSE=<file>` makes the OpenRouter adapter read a saved response body (a JSON file whose top-level `http_status` and `body` fields stand in for the HTTP reply) instead of calling the network; `READERS_CANNED_CODEX=<dir>` makes the GPT adapter read a saved `events.jsonl`, `output.md`, `stderr.txt`, and `exit` from that directory instead of launching a child. The canned files live at `plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/fixtures/canned/`.
Acceptance criteria:
- AC1: DeepSeek and Qwen each complete a live fixture read — verify: manual: one authorized run per row, profile `starved`; both results show status `ok`, a non-empty `raw_text`, a `generation_id`, a `response_raw` path whose file parses as JSON, and the parity line `no :online suffix`; recorded in `docs/evidence/readers/slice-b-openrouter-runs.md`, and the deepseek run's `response.raw` is copied to `docs/evidence/readers/slice-b-deepseek-response.raw` (a response body carries no credential) for Slice G AC5; `grep -c 'sk-or-' docs/evidence/readers/slice-b-openrouter-runs.md docs/evidence/readers/slice-b-deepseek-response.raw` prints `0` for both.
- AC2: Guard mapping is deterministic — verify: with `READERS_TEST=1`, five canned OpenRouter bodies (finish reason `length`; empty content; finish reason `tool_calls`; `http_status` 500 with an error body; finish reason `length` with empty content) return `incomplete`, `empty`, `transport-failed`, `transport-failed`, `incomplete` respectively, each `incomplete` case's partial text present at `<diagnostics>/partial.md` and absent from `raw.md` and `raw_text`; two canned Codex directories (an event stream carrying a truncation signal; an empty `output.md` with exit 0) return `incomplete` and `empty`; without `READERS_TEST=1` each hook returns `transport-failed` with the refusal reason.
- AC3: A missing credential is lane-unavailable — verify: `env -u OPENROUTER_API_KEY plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers <request for deepseek>` exits non-zero with status `lane-unavailable` and `ls <run dir>/<call id>` prints `sidecar.json` alone.
- AC4: Oversize counts the whole prompt — verify: a document sized so that the document alone fits the `qwen` row's window (1,000,000) minus the output budget but the document plus a 4,000-byte mandate does not returns `oversize` with the measured estimate and the limit; no `dispatch.log`.
- AC5: Memory round-trips — verify: with `READERS_CHECKOUT` pointing at a scratch clone of the checkout (`git clone --depth 1 ~/Developer/tony-skills <scratch>` or a `cp -R` of it), run a `deepseek` read with `model: deepseek/deepseek-v4-pro` (the id the callers pin today, live on OpenRouter); `python3 -c "import json;print(json.load(open('<scratch>/plugins/readers/last-picks.json'))['picks']['deepseek']['model'])"` prints that id; `readers suggest deepseek --run R2` prints the same id as the suggestion and marks the row outside.
- AC6: A roster bump clears the pick — verify: in the scratch clone, change the `deepseek` row's `model` in `<scratch>/plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/roster.json`, then run the scratch clone's own entry point `<scratch>/plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers suggest deepseek --run R3` with `READERS_CHECKOUT=<scratch>`; the suggestion is the new roster default and the output carries the drop note; the scratch memory's `deepseek` entry is marked dropped.
- AC7: The freeze holds from suggest and survives a concurrent first call — verify: (a) `readers suggest deepseek --run R4` (snapshot created), then change the scratch memory's `deepseek` entry by hand, then dispatch two authorized calls with run id R4 concurrently; both sidecars name the R4 snapshot, both resolved models equal the suggested one, and `ls <run dir>/snapshot` shows one snapshot; (b) with no prior suggest, dispatch two calls with run id R5 concurrently; `ls <run dir>/snapshot` shows one snapshot and both sidecars name it.
- AC8: Memory unavailable is honest — verify: `READERS_CHECKOUT=/nonexistent plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers suggest qwen --run R6` prints the roster default (read from the checkout's own roster beside the script) with `memory: unavailable`, and `find <run dir> /nonexistent "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/readers" -name last-picks.json 2>/dev/null` prints nothing.
- AC9: The tracked memory file exists and carries no test residue — verify: `git show HEAD:plugins/readers/last-picks.json` prints `{"protocol_version": 1, "picks": {}}` (whitespace aside) and `git status --short plugins/readers/last-picks.json` prints nothing at the end of the slice.
- AC10: Concurrent picks are not lost — verify: two authorized calls with explicit picks on `deepseek` and `qwen` launched together against the scratch clone; afterwards the scratch memory holds both entries.
- AC11: Secrets stay out — verify: AC8 of Slice A repeated on the slice's tree and evidence, with `grep -rc 'Authorization' <AC1 deepseek diagnostics dir>` printing `0` for every file (this lane is the one that sends the header).
Footprint: plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/readers.py; plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/contract.md (the suggest step, the hooks, the memory and snapshot fields); plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/roster.json (the verified reasoning parameter in the two rows' quirks); plugins/readers/last-picks.json (created); plugins/readers/skills/readers/assets/fixtures/canned/ (the seven canned cases); docs/evidence/readers/slice-b-openrouter-runs.md; docs/evidence/readers/slice-b-deepseek-response.raw
Not in this slice: host lanes, the SKILL.md, callers, guides, any write to the tracked `last-picks.json` beyond its creation.
Depends on: Slice A
Status: signed off

## Slice C — the skill body, the host lanes, and the install
Goal: `/readers` exists as a plugin: callers summon it by name and it runs any roster row, the Claude and Gemini lanes from the skill body and the portable lanes through the runner, with one result shape and the runner's pre-send checks on every call; Tony can also type it for an ad hoc read. Proven from the checkout before merge; re-proven installed in Slice I.
Requirements:
- R1: `plugins/readers/skills/readers/SKILL.md` with a frontmatter description that names the summon form and the direct form, and a short body (under 120 lines). The body carries no model id, effort value, window, budget, or transport parameter; those live only in `roster.json` and `contract.md`, which the body references by `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/readers/assets/...`. The body states the protocol version it implements and prints it once per run, before any dispatch, as the line `READERS-PROTOCOL: 1`.
- R2: The summon interface: a caller invokes `/readers` (the Skill tool) with a request block whose fields are the contract's; a request may be one call or a fleet (several calls sharing `run_id` and the authorization, each with its own `row`, `mandate`, `documents` or `workspace`, `profile`, `call_id`, and `raw_path`, launched together — blueprint Q5). Every call, host or portable, is first resolved by `suggest --run` (the first call freezes the run) and then passes the runner's pre-send checks on the resolved request (`readers validate -`), so the model the checks saw is the model dispatched; a failing check refuses the call with the contract's status. Portable rows are then executed by running `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/readers/assets/readers` (in the background for fleets); host rows by the skill body per R3 and R4, which then hand the result to the runner's `record` step so the sidecar and `raw.md` are written the same way. For every call the body reports one chat line in the fixed form `READERS: <row id> · <status> · <effective model id> · <raw path | none> · <sidecar path>` followed by the result JSON; callers key on that line and never on the version line. A second summon form, `/readers suggest <row id>[,<row id>...] --run <run id>`, makes no call: it runs the runner's `suggest` step and reports its lines, so a caller can build its ask (R6).
- R3: The Claude lane: a fresh subagent that receives only the mandate and the documents (plus `REVIEW.md` when a caller passes it as a document), never this session's conversation, reasoning, or prior findings; the mandate is prefixed with a fixed instruction that it may use no web tool under any profile, may not summon `/readers` or spawn agents, must report everything it finds and never self-censor (signoff SKILL.md:68), and, under `starved` and `packet-only`, may read no files and use no tools. Under `starved` and `packet-only` the call runs through the Workflow route (assumption 9) so the parity line records the harness's per-agent tool-call count, `toolCalls: 0` required; under `repo` it may read the workspace and under `repo-with-tools` may run tests with writes confined to scratch and ignored caches, never a tracked file, reporting "verification blocked" when the sandbox stops an execution it needed; a `repo-with-tools` request carrying `isolation: worktree` launches the reviewer in the Agent tool's worktree isolation (signoff SKILL.md:66's rule for lenses whose method mutates the checkout) and the sidecar's `isolation` field says `worktree`; the parity line for those two profiles is `web tools forbidden by instruction`. `claude-session` inherits the session model; the body fills `session_model` with the session's model id (the id the harness reports for the running session) on every `claude-session` request, so the runner's floor check has its input; a floor-bound request with no `session_model` is `floor-refused` with reason `session model unknown`. `claude-fable` and `claude-opus` pin the model. Effort is pinned through the Workflow route when the caller passes one, for pinned rows as well. The raw capture is the subagent's final message.
- R4: The Gemini lane: `mcp__antigravity__ask_gemini` with the row's model id, the composed prompt (produced by the runner's `compose` step so the two never drift), `cwd` per the access profile (a fresh empty directory for `starved`, the packet directory for `packet-only`, the workspace for `repo`), `skip_permissions` omitted (the tool's default `false` is its only real write guard, per `tools/antigravity-mcp/README.md`), `effort` never passed when the row encodes it in the model name. An empty response is a failed run whatever the status flag says; a non-empty, complete, mandated-format response under an error status is `ok` with the anomaly recorded (vertical SKILL.md:58's rule, qualifier included); partial text or a diagnostic string under an error status is `transport-failed` with the text kept in diagnostics. The parity line is the roster row's entry for the profile used.
- R5: Ask policy: when summoned by a caller, `readers` asks Tony nothing about lanes; it trusts the caller's `authorized` flag for outside rows and refuses without it. The direct form, `/readers <row id> <document path> "<one-line mandate>" [<profile>]`, runs under `starved` unless the fourth argument names a profile, asks once for the word when the row is outside (an invocation that already carries it, such as "send it", counts), then runs, prints the raw output in chat, and files a copy only when Tony names a path.
- R6: Before a caller's ask, the caller summons `/readers suggest` for each requested row so its own ask line carries the remembered pick and any drop note, and the run is frozen from that moment; `readers` never runs the ask itself.
- R7: Version gate: a caller states `readers-protocol: 1` in its SKILL.md and passes it as `protocol_version`; the runner's pre-send check refuses a mismatch with `version-mismatch` before any dispatch, and the body's first line lets a human see the installed version.

Precon's four standing prohibitions on invoking a skill (SKILL.md:57 "it never invokes a skill", :89 "never auto-invokes /blueprint (or any other skill)", :99 rule 7 "No invoking", :122 "Don't invoke any skill, ever") each gain the carve-out "except `/readers`, the loop's reader component, for the exit test"; the rest of each rule stands.
- R2: architect `plugins/architect/skills/architect/SKILL.md`, Step 6 (lines 100–116): the blind review's lanes become roster rows (GPT default, Gemini, Claude on Tony's naming) with the `/readers suggest` result shown at the ask; each reviewer is one `/readers` call with the fixed architect instruction as the mandate, the scope doc as the single document, profile `starved`; architect keeps its verbatim save paths and collision suffixes (line 114), its HTML-unescape rule as the only transformation (line 113), its failed-review reporting (`Review: failed — <reason>` from the readers status), and its retry-only-on-Tony's-word rule. Architect's three never-invoke rules (SKILL.md:14 "it never invokes another loop skill", with its one sanctioned exception; :126 "no other loop skill invoked"; :153 "don't invoke any loop skill") each gain "except `/readers` for the blind review" beside the existing exception.

## Slice H — signoff, wargame, recheck converted
Goal: The three reviewer skills spawn their fresh Claude reviewers through `/readers` with a narrow reviewer mandate, the Opus floor, and the `repo-with-tools` profile, keeping every independence, isolation, and verdict rule they have today.
Requirements:
- R1: signoff `plugins/signoff/skills/signoff/SKILL.md` Step 0 (lines 14–18) and the reviewer mechanics (lines 42–70, the launch paragraph at line 66): the floor is passed to readers as `opus` with `session_model` filled, and the STOP-below-floor rule stays signoff's; each lens is one `claude-session` call in a `/readers` fleet with a narrow reviewer mandate (the lens task, the scope, the spec path, the finding shape, and `REVIEW.md` as a document when present; never signoff's own orchestration text), profile `repo-with-tools`, no `raw_path` (signoff's verdict doc remains the record, built from `raw_text`); the "reviewers do not spawn subagents" and "report everything, never self-censor" rules (line 68) are met by readers' fixed instruction; the declare-the-method rule (line 90) records "verification blocked" when a reviewer reports it. The rest of line 66's paragraph is carried verbatim into the rewritten paragraph: a lens whose method must mutate the checkout (migrations run twice, `seams`) passes `isolation: worktree` on its call (the Agent tool's worktree isolation, Slice C R3), or the Method line declares the lens exercised committed state only; and the one-suite-at-a-time rule for device-bound suites stands as written today. Lines 46 (the ledger split), 52–60 (LEAN/DEEP/LIGHT and the lens sets), 62 (`REVIEW.md` passes to lenses), and 64 (add-a-lens) are not changed.
- R2: recheck `plugins/recheck/skills/recheck/SKILL.md` Step 0 (lines 14–16) and Step 2 (the reviewer sentence at line 34): the one fresh reviewer is one `claude-session` call, floor `opus`, `session_model` filled, profile `repo-with-tools`, mandate = the checklist items with their file:line, claim, and failure scenario, plus `REVIEW.md` when present. "Verification blocked" is recorded inside recheck's existing vocabulary, not as a third outcome: the item resolves to `not fixed` and its how-verified field reads `verification blocked: <what the sandbox stopped>`, so lines 38–41, 55, and 77 keep their `fixed | not fixed | broke:` forms and /ship's open-filter and Status mapping (lines 26, 53) see an ordinary open item.
- R3: wargame `plugins/wargame/skills/wargame/SKILL.md` Step 0 (lines 10–16) and the FULL fan-out (lines 51–62, the launch sentence at line 53): the 4–6 parallel adversary agents are one `/readers` fleet of `claude-session` calls, floor `opus`, `session_model` filled, profile `repo-with-tools`, each with its lens as the mandate; a reviewer's "verification blocked" is recorded in the verification log; hand-verification of headliners stays the session's.
- R4: The AGENTS.md independence invariant holds by construction: reviewers receive only mandate and documents, never the author's rationale. Each skill states `readers-protocol: 1` and passes it.
Acceptance criteria:
- AC1: The three skills summon readers with the floor and profile — verify: for each of the three files, `grep -c '/readers' <file>` prints 1 or more, `grep -c 'repo-with-tools' <file>` prints 1 or more, `grep -c 'readers-protocol: 1' <file>` prints 1 or more, and `grep -c 'opus' <file>` prints 1 or more.

### 2026-09-07 — build: slice I
- readers, Agent route (`repo` / `repo-with-tools`): the harness hands every reviewer the workspace's `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, the user's global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` with `_about-me.md`, and the repo's auto-memory index as system text, so `contract.md:76`'s "the composed prompt and nothing else" is false there; `compose` records `workdir_instruction_files` and no caller reports it (R5(d)'s signoff MAJOR 1, all four transcripts) — a readers and caller change for a later word
- readers, Workflow route under `starved` / `packet-only`: the harness's git-status block reaches the subagent; precon's reader cited the latest commit message and architect's reader "the git log in my session context" — a warmth leak on the lanes whose isolation label reads `harness-enforced (toolCalls: 0)`; the label measures tool use, not what the harness injects
- readers, Agent route: the Agent tool appends a harness trailer (agentId, usage) after the subagent's final message; recheck's session excluded it from `capture.md` on its own reading and said so in a SKILL NOTE; the contract's "the subagent's final message" does not say
- readers: a `general-purpose` reviewer carries outside-model and outbound MCP tools (`ask_gemini`, `codex`, gmail send, github push/merge, shortcuts) that neither the fixed prefix nor any mandate forbids (R5(d)'s signoff MAJOR 2)
- precon: a ledger source of the form "answered question N" cannot be recovered on re-invocation (precon's own SKILL NOTE in R5(b))
- architect: the skill has no stated fallback for a session without the Artifact tool; the R5(e) session improvised the page-beside-the-doc and an `Artifact: none` line
- The R5(d) demonstration signoff charged two MAJORs and sixteen MINORs to Slice H's texts on the plan's ledger while H's card stays `signed off` per R5(d); the card and the record disagree until Tony rules on the verdict's three questions
- Running (e) and (f) concurrently in one scratch repo made each session notice the other's untracked writes mid-run (both reported it, neither was affected); sequential runs would avoid the noise
- readers, Agent route (`repo` / `repo-with-tools`): `compose` returns `prompt_param: prompt`, so the session must emit the whole composed prompt as one tool parameter; a document set past the session's single-message output capacity (this plan, 440,648 bytes, inlined by inspect's repo-reality lens) is undispatchable on that route while the Workflow route carries its prompt in a script file (R5(c) attempt 2, sidecar reason quoted in the evidence file); inspect's packet rule and its `repo` pin for repo-reality collide on any doc of this size, and the same holds for any caller handing a large document to a `repo`-profile Claude call — a readers change (a file hand-off for Agent-route prompts, or a Workflow-with-workspace route) for a later word
- The headless `claude -p` sessions that ran the proofs reported `claude-fable-5-1` as their session model for (a), (b), (d), (e), (f); the (c) attempt after the usage-limit reset reported `claude-opus-5[1m]`, so a headless session's model is not fixed and a caller's `session_model` line is the only record of which ran
- The account's session usage limit ended one headless proof mid-run with a single output line and left the run's composed Workflow scripts under the repo's ignored `.readers/<run id>/` (nothing ran `record`); worth a line in the evidence convention: check `.readers/` after any headless reader run
- inspect: the stamp insert (directly after `Out of scope:`) shifts every later line by one, so the punch-list block's own `<path>:<line>` citations written first go stale; the R5(c) take-2 session rebased them by hand and asked for a pinned write order in Step 5 (its SKILL NOTE, 2026-09-08)
